<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that a nonzero effective plane curve of degree <i>d</i> at <i>r</i> ≥ 10 very general complex points has total multiplicity strictly less than $`d\sqrt r`$. The inequality holds simultaneously for all curves, including reducible and nonreduced curves, and establishes Nagata's conjecture in its strict, nonhomogeneous form.

- **C01** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every integer $r\ge10$, there is a countable union $E_r$ of proper Zariski-closed subsets of $U_r$, with nonempty complement, such that the following holds for every tuple $(p_1,\ldots,p_r)\notin E_r$. If $C$ is a nonzero effective plane curve of degree $d\ge1$ and $m_1,\ldots,m_r\in\mathbb Z_{\ge0}$ satisfy $\operatorname{mult}_{p_i}C\ge m_i$, then $$d\sqrt r>\sum_{i=1}^r m_i.$$
  - Explanation: The strict square-root degree inequality holds simultaneously for effective plane curves and nonnegative multiplicity vectors outside a single exceptional countable union.
- **C02** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-universal)): For each $r\ge10$ there is a countable union $E_r$ of proper Zariski-closed subsets of $U_r$, all defined over $\mathbb Q$, with $U_r\setminus E_r\ne\varnothing$, having the following property. If a tuple outside $E_r$ supports a degree-$d_0$ form, $d_0\ge1$, with multiplicities at least $m_1,\ldots,m_r\in\mathbb Z_{\ge0}$ and $$d_0\sqrt r\le\sum_{i=1}^r m_i,$$ then $\mathsf U_r(d,m)$ holds for some positive integers $d,m$ with $d/m\le\sqrt r$. If $\mathsf U_r(d,m)$ holds, then $\mathsf U_r(Nd,Nm)$ holds for every positive integer $N$. Consequently, to prove Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> for a fixed $r$, it suffices to rule out every $\mathsf U_r(d,m)$ with $d/m\le\sqrt r$.
  - Explanation: Closed incidence loci and cyclic products reduce a violation to a universal equal-multiplicity system, and powers preserve universal existence.
- **C03** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-normal-polynomial)): Suppose that $\mathsf U_r(d,m)$ holds. For every choice of distinct points $a_1,\ldots,a_r\in B$ and arbitrary vectors $c_i\in M_{a_i}$, there is a nonzero polynomial $$\label{eq:normal-polynomial} F(w)=\sum_{j=0}^{\lfloor d/k\rfloor}f_jw^j, \qquad f_j\in H^0(B,L^{d-kj}),$$ having multiplicity at least $m$ at each $(a_i,c_i)\in\operatorname{Tot}(M)$. Its coefficients may be taken to be algebraic sections.
  - Explanation: Specializing a universal system toward a smooth plane curve produces a nonzero normal polynomial with the prescribed multiplicities at arbitrary normal displacements.
- **C04** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-squares)): Let $r=k^2$ with $k\ge4$. There are no positive integers $d,m$ such that $d\le km$ and $\mathsf U_r(d,m)$ holds.
  - Explanation: For square point counts, section degrees and a choice outside the evaluation image exclude universal systems at or below the critical slope.
- **C05** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cubic-low-degree)): If $r\ge10$ and $\mathsf U_r(d,m)$ holds for positive integers $d,m$, then $d>3m$.
  - Explanation: Zero displacements on a cubic force the degree to exceed the cubic multiplicity threshold.
- **C06** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-necessary-w)): Suppose $r\geq10$, $d>3m$, and $\mathsf U_r(d,m)$ holds. Choose $B,P,A$ and $\mathcal W$ as above, and fix a holomorphic frame of $A$ on a coordinate disk in $B\setminus P$. For every choice of $q=r-9$ distinct points $b_1,\ldots,b_q$ in this disk, there is a nonzero $\widetilde F\in\mathcal W$ of multiplicity at least $m$ at each point $(b_\alpha,1)$ of $\operatorname{Tot}(A)$, where $1$ is the scalar coordinate in the chosen frame.
  - Explanation: Removing prescribed coefficient zeros transports the remaining multiplicity conditions into a polynomial section space on a degree-zero bundle.
- **C07** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-torus-sections)): For $n>0$ and $\gamma\in\mathbb C^*$, the space $H^0(X_\tau,D(n,\gamma))$ has dimension $n$. The Laurent coefficients of a section $f(z)=\sum_{K\in\mathbb Z}c_Kz^K$ satisfy $$\begin{split} c_{K+n}&=\gamma^{-1}\tau^Kc_K,\\ c_{K+np}&=c_K\gamma^{-p} \tau^{pK+np(p-1)/2}\qquad(p\in\mathbb Z). \end{split} \label{eq:laurent-recurrence}$$ One coefficient in each residue class modulo $n$ may be prescribed arbitrarily. Taking one nonzero starting coefficient at a time gives a basis supported in the individual residue classes.
  - Explanation: The Laurent recurrence gives a section basis with one independent coefficient in each residue class.
- **C08** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-cubic-embedding)): For every $\gamma\in\mathbb C^*$, the complete space of sections of $D(3,\gamma)$ embeds $X_\tau$ into $\mathbb P^2$ as a smooth irreducible plane cubic, with $\mathcal O_{\mathbb P^2}(1)$ pulling back to $D(3,\gamma)$.
  - Explanation: The complete section space separates points and tangent directions and realizes the torus as a smooth plane cubic.
- **C09** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-collision)): If $\mathsf U_r(d,m)$ holds, then $\ker J_\tau\neq\{0\}$ for every fixed $\tau\in(0,1)$.
  - Explanation: Colliding points on each fixed surface yields a nonzero section satisfying the limiting jet conditions.
- **C10** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-theta-limit)): Fix an integer $n>0$, a real number $\sigma$, and a complex number $\epsilon$ with $\|\epsilon\|=1$. Put $U=\sigma-x_0n$ and assume $U\notin\mathbb Z$. For each integer $K$ with $U<K<U+n$, there is a section $s_{K,\tau}$ of $D(n,\epsilon\tau^\sigma)$ obtained from the Laurent recurrence <a href="#eq:laurent-recurrence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:laurent-recurrence">[eq:laurent-recurrence]</a> by setting the coefficient of $z^K$ equal to $\tau^{-x_0K}$ and all other residue classes modulo $n$ equal to zero. These $n$ sections form a basis. In the local covering-space frame their expressions are $$s_{K,\tau}(z_0e^x) =\sum_{p\in\mathbb Z}\epsilon^{-p} \tau^{p(K-U)+np(p-1)/2}e^{(K+np)x} \longrightarrow e^{Kx}. \label{eq:normalized-theta}$$ The convergence is uniform on each compact subset of the complex $x$-plane, and the same is true after any fixed number of $x$-derivatives. Moreover, $$\Pi(z_0e^x)\longrightarrow1$$ with the same uniform holomorphic and derivative convergence.
  - Explanation: Normalized theta bases converge with all fixed derivatives to exponentials while the product removing prescribed zeros converges to the constant section.
- **C11** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-matrix-limit)): Choose fixed orderings of $\mathcal I$ and $\mathcal S$, and represent $J_\tau$ in the basis $E_{j,K,\tau}$ by the matrix $B_\tau$. These matrices have one fixed size and converge entrywise as $\tau\to0^+$ to the matrix $$B_0=\bigl(K^\ell j^b\bigr)_ {\substack{(\ell,b)\in\mathcal I\\(j,K)\in\mathcal S}}. \label{eq:limiting-matrix}$$ If $B_0$ has full column rank, then $J_\tau$ is injective for all sufficiently small positive $\tau$.
  - Explanation: Mixed derivative convergence gives a monomial evaluation matrix; a nonzero maximal minor implies injectivity for sufficiently small positive degeneration parameters.
- **C12** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-interpolation)): Let $q,m$ be positive integers and $S\subset\mathbb C^2$ a finite set, with coordinates $(j,K)$. For each represented height $K$, let $N_K$ be its row size. Suppose $$N_K\le m,\qquad \#\{K:N_K\ge t\}\le q(m-t+1) \quad(1\le t\le m,\ t\in\mathbb Z).$$ Then every function $S\to\mathbb C$ is the restriction of a polynomial in $$\mathcal P_{q,m} =\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb C} \bigl\{K^\ell J^b:0\le b<m,\quad0\le\ell<q(m-b)\bigr\} \subset\mathbb C[J,K].$$ Here evaluation at $(j,K)$ substitutes $J=j$.
  - Explanation: Treating horizontal rows in decreasing size and adding corrections that vanish on previous rows interpolates every prescribed function under the stated row bounds.
- **C13** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-exponent-enclosure)): Every pair $(j,K)\in\mathcal S$ satisfies $$\label{eq:exponent-polygon} \begin{aligned} 0&\le X\le1+\rho/9,\\ -aX+\Delta(X-1)_+ &\le Y\le-aX+\rho+(\Delta-9)(X-1)_+. \end{aligned}$$ The region in <a href="#eq:exponent-polygon" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:exponent-polygon">[eq:exponent-polygon]</a> is contained in $\mathcal D_\lambda$. Consequently, $\mathcal S\subseteq m\mathcal D_\lambda+(0,U_0)$.
  - Explanation: The exponent intervals and the degree-zero endpoint lie in the scaled and translated contracting polygon.
- **C14** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-slice-profile)): We have $\mathcal D_\lambda=\lambda\mathcal D_1$. The nonempty horizontal slices of $\mathcal D_1$ have lengths $$w(Y)= \begin{cases} 1-Y/a,&0\le Y\le a,\\[1mm] 1+Y/\bigl(a(1+a/9)\bigr), &-a(1+a/9)\le Y\le0. \end{cases}$$ Their maximum length is one. For $0\le s\le m\lambda$, the heights of nonempty slices of $m\mathcal D_\lambda+(0,U_0)$ of length at least $s$ form the closed interval $$\bigl[ U_0-a(1+a/9)(m\lambda-s),\; U_0+a(m\lambda-s) \bigr],$$ whose length is $q(m\lambda-s)$.
  - Explanation: The polygon has an explicit horizontal width profile whose superlevel heights form a closed interval.
- **C15** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-integer-rows)): If $q(1-\lambda)m\ge1$, then the exponent set satisfies $$\label{eq:row-count} \begin{aligned} N_K&\le m &&(K\in\mathbb Z),\\ \#\{K\in\mathbb Z:N_K\ge t\} &\le q(m-t+1) &&(1\le t\le m,\ t\in\mathbb Z). \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: The scaling condition absorbs the integer endpoint contribution and supplies the row bounds required for interpolation.
- **C16** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-full-rank)): If $q(1-\lambda)m\ge1$, then the limiting matrix $B_0$ of <a href="#eq:limiting-matrix" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:limiting-matrix">[eq:limiting-matrix]</a> has full column rank. Consequently, $J_\tau$ is injective for all sufficiently small positive $\tau$.
  - Explanation: Row interpolation gives full column rank, hence injectivity contradicts the collision kernel after a common power supplies the scaling condition.
- **C17** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-fields)): Let $k$ be an uncountable algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, and let $r\ge10$. There is a countable union of proper Zariski-closed subsets of the space of distinct ordered $r$-tuples in $\mathbb P^2_k$, with nonempty complement, outside which the conclusion of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> holds with the same degree and multiplicity conventions.
  - Explanation: Rational incidence minors transfer the simultaneous inequality to uncountable algebraically closed characteristic-zero fields.
- **C18** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-sesha)): The class $N_r=\sqrt r\,H-\sum_iE_i$ is strictly nef and satisfies $N_r^2=0$. In particular, $$\varepsilon(\mathcal O(1);p_1,\ldots,p_r)=\frac1{\sqrt r}.$$
  - Explanation: The theorem supplies a strictly nef square-zero divisor class and determines the multipoint Seshadri constant.

```json
          "exact_quote": "If $C$ is a nonzero effective plane curve of degree $d\\ge1$ and $m_1,\\ldots,m_r\\in\\mathbb Z_{\\ge0}$ satisfy $\\operatorname{mult}_{p_i}C\\ge m_i$",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-039.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "C01",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
          "exact_quote": "For every integer $r\\ge10$, there is a countable union $E_r$ of proper Zariski-closed subsets of $U_r$, with nonempty complement, such that the following holds for every tuple $(p_1,\\ldots,p_r)\\notin E_r$. If $C$ is a nonzero effective plane curve of degree $d\\ge1$ and $m_1,\\ldots,m_r\\in\\mathbb Z_{\\ge0}$ satisfy $\\operatorname{mult}_{p_i}C\\ge m_i$, then $$d\\sqrt r>\\sum_{i=1}^r m_i.$$",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-039.1"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-thm-main"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-universal",
        "exact_quote": "For each $r\\ge10$ there is a countable union $E_r$ of proper Zariski-closed subsets of $U_r$, all defined over $\\mathbb Q$, with $U_r\\setminus E_r\\ne\\varnothing$, having the following property. If a tuple outside $E_r$ supports a degree-$d_0$ form, $d_0\\ge1$, with multiplicities at least $m_1,\\ldots,m_r\\in\\mathbb Z_{\\ge0}$ and $$d_0\\sqrt r\\le\\sum_{i=1}^r m_i,$$ then $\\mathsf U_r(d,m)$ holds for some positive integers $d,m$ with $d/m\\le\\sqrt r$. If $\\mathsf U_r(d,m)$ holds, then $\\mathsf U_r(Nd,Nm)$ holds for every positive integer $N$. Consequently, to prove Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a> for a fixed $r$, it suffices to rule out every $\\mathsf U_r(d,m)$ with $d/m\\le\\sqrt r$.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-039.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "For $n>0$ and $\\gamma\\in\\mathbb C^*$, the space $H^0(X_\\tau,D(n,\\gamma))$ has dimension $n$. The Laurent coefficients of a section $f(z)=\\sum_{K\\in\\mathbb Z}c_Kz^K$ satisfy $$\\begin{split}\n c_{K+n}&=\\gamma^{-1}\\tau^Kc_K,\\\\\n c_{K+np}&=c_K\\gamma^{-p}\n       \\tau^{pK+np(p-1)/2}\\qquad(p\\in\\mathbb Z).\n\\end{split}\n\\label{eq:laurent-recurrence}$$ One coefficient in each residue class modulo $n$ may be prescribed arbitrarily. Taking one nonzero starting coefficient at a time gives a basis supported in the individual residue classes.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-039.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-torus-sections",
          "exact_quote": "For $n>0$ and $\\gamma\\in\\mathbb C^*$, the space $H^0(X_\\tau,D(n,\\gamma))$ has dimension $n$. Taking one nonzero starting coefficient at a time gives a basis supported in the individual residue classes.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-039.1"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-theta-limit",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $n>0$, a real number $\\sigma$, and a complex number $\\epsilon$ with $|\\epsilon|=1$. Put $U=\\sigma-x_0n$ and assume $U\\notin\\mathbb Z$. For each integer $K$ with $U<K<U+n$, there is a section $s_{K,\\tau}$ of $D(n,\\epsilon\\tau^\\sigma)$ obtained from the Laurent recurrence <a href=\"#eq:laurent-recurrence\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:laurent-recurrence\">[eq:laurent-recurrence]</a> by setting the coefficient of $z^K$ equal to $\\tau^{-x_0K}$ and all other residue classes modulo $n$ equal to zero. These $n$ sections form a basis. In the local covering-space frame their expressions are $$s_{K,\\tau}(z_0e^x)\n  =\\sum_{p\\in\\mathbb Z}\\epsilon^{-p}\n     \\tau^{p(K-U)+np(p-1)/2}e^{(K+np)x}\n  \\longrightarrow e^{Kx}.\n \\label{eq:normalized-theta}$$ The convergence is uniform on each compact subset of the complex $x$-plane, and the same is true after any fixed number of $x$-derivatives. Moreover, $$\\Pi(z_0e^x)\\longrightarrow1$$ with the same uniform holomorphic and derivative convergence.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-039.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-theta-limit",

Moreover, $$\\Pi(z_0e^x)\\longrightarrow1$$ with the same uniform holomorphic and derivative convergence.",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-theta-limit",
          "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $n>0$, a real number $\\sigma$, and a complex number $\\epsilon$ with $|\\epsilon|=1$. Put $U=\\sigma-x_0n$ and assume $U\\notin\\mathbb Z$. For each integer $K$ with $U<K<U+n$, there is a section $s_{K,\\tau}$ of $D(n,\\epsilon\\tau^\\sigma)$ obtained from the Laurent recurrence <a href=\"#eq:laurent-recurrence\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:laurent-recurrence\">[eq:laurent-recurrence]</a> by setting the coefficient of $z^K$ equal to $\\tau^{-x_0K}$ and all other residue classes modulo $n$ equal to zero. These $n$ sections form a basis. In the local covering-space frame their expressions are $$s_{K,\\tau}(z_0e^x)\n  =\\sum_{p\\in\\mathbb Z}\\epsilon^{-p}\n     \\tau^{p(K-U)+np(p-1)/2}e^{(K+np)x}\n  \\longrightarrow e^{Kx}.\n \\label{eq:normalized-theta}$$ The convergence is uniform on each compact subset of the complex $x$-plane, and the same is true after any fixed number of $x$-derivatives. Moreover, $$\\Pi(z_0e^x)\\longrightarrow1$$ with the same uniform holomorphic and derivative convergence.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-039.1"
        }
```

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Distinct-point configuration space** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Let $$U_r=\{(p_1,\ldots,p_r)\in(\mathbb P^2_\mathbb C)^r:p_i\ne p_j\text{ for }i\ne j\}.$$
- **Nonzero effective plane curve** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A nonzero effective plane curve is the zero divisor of a nonzero homogeneous form of degree $d\ge1$.
- **Point multiplicity** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Its multiplicity at a point is the order of that form in the regular local ring.
- **Very general points** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The phrase *very general* in the following theorem means the complement of one countable union of proper closed subsets, chosen simultaneously for all degrees and multiplicity vectors.
- **Order of a section** ([source](#sec:reductions)): The order of a section is the order of its scalar expression in a local frame.
- **Taylor vanishing criterion** ([source](#sec:reductions)): In local coordinates, order at least $m$ means that every Taylor coefficient of total degree less than $m$ vanishes.
- **Universal equal-multiplicity system** ([source](#pm-h-6)): For positive integers $d,m$, write $\mathsf U_r(d,m)$ for the property $$\label{eq:universal} \begin{gathered} \text{For every }(p_1,\ldots,p_r)\in U_r\text{ there is a nonzero form}\\ S\in H^0(\mathbb P^2,\mathcal O_{\mathbb P^2}(d)) \quad\text{such that}\quad \operatorname{mult}_{p_i}(S)\ge m\quad(1\le i\le r). \end{gathered}$$
- **Multiplier line bundle** ([source](#pm-h-12)): For $n\in\mathbb Z$ and $\gamma\in\mathbb C^*$, define $D(n,\gamma)$ as the holomorphic line bundle obtained from $\mathbb C^*\times\mathbb C$ by the action generated by $$(z,t)\longmapsto(\tau z,\gamma z^{-n}t).$$
- **Nef and strictly nef classes** ([source](#sec:consequences)): A real divisor class is *nef* if its intersection with every irreducible curve is nonnegative, and *strictly nef* if every such intersection is positive.
- **Multipoint Seshadri constant** ([source](#sec:consequences)): The multipoint Seshadri constant of the line bundle $\mathcal O_{\mathbb P^2}(1)$ at the tuple is $$\varepsilon(\mathcal O(1);p_1,\ldots,p_r) =\sup\{t\ge0:H-t\textstyle\sum_iE_i\text{ is nef}\}.$$

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R01** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-full-rank-2): The strict square-root degree inequality holds simultaneously for effective plane curves and nonnegative multiplicity vectors outside a single exceptional countable union. -> The conclusion of the source statement.
- **R02** [claim](#stmt-lem-universal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-universal): Closed incidence loci and cyclic products reduce a violation to a universal equal-multiplicity system, and powers preserve universal existence. -> The conclusion of the source statement.
- **R03** [claim](#stmt-prop-normal-polynomial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-normal-polynomial): Specializing a universal system toward a smooth plane curve produces a nonzero normal polynomial with the prescribed multiplicities at arbitrary normal displacements. -> The conclusion of the source statement.
- **R04** [claim](#stmt-prop-squares) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-squares): For square point counts, section degrees and a choice outside the evaluation image exclude universal systems at or below the critical slope. -> The conclusion of the source statement.
- **R05** [claim](#stmt-lem-cubic-low-degree) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cubic-low-degree): Zero displacements on a cubic force the degree to exceed the cubic multiplicity threshold. -> The conclusion of the source statement.
- **R06** [claim](#stmt-prop-necessary-w) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-necessary-w): Removing prescribed coefficient zeros transports the remaining multiplicity conditions into a polynomial section space on a degree-zero bundle. -> The conclusion of the source statement.
- **R07** [claim](#stmt-lem-torus-sections) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-torus-sections): The Laurent recurrence gives a section basis with one independent coefficient in each residue class. -> The conclusion of the source statement.
- **R08** [claim](#stmt-lem-cubic-embedding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cubic-embedding): The complete section space separates points and tangent directions and realizes the torus as a smooth plane cubic. -> The conclusion of the source statement.
- **R09** [claim](#stmt-lem-collision) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-collision): Colliding points on each fixed surface yields a nonzero section satisfying the limiting jet conditions. -> The conclusion of the source statement.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-lem-theta-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-theta-limit): Normalized theta bases converge with all fixed derivatives to exponentials while the product removing prescribed zeros converges to the constant section. -> The conclusion of the source statement.
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-prop-matrix-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-matrix-limit): Mixed derivative convergence gives a monomial evaluation matrix; a nonzero maximal minor implies injectivity for sufficiently small positive degeneration parameters. -> The conclusion of the source statement.
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-lem-interpolation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-interpolation): Treating horizontal rows in decreasing size and adding corrections that vanish on previous rows interpolates every prescribed function under the stated row bounds. -> The conclusion of the source statement.
- **R13** [claim](#stmt-lem-exponent-enclosure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exponent-enclosure): The exponent intervals and the degree-zero endpoint lie in the scaled and translated contracting polygon. -> The conclusion of the source statement.
- **R14** [claim](#stmt-lem-slice-profile) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-slice-profile): The polygon has an explicit horizontal width profile whose superlevel heights form a closed interval. -> The conclusion of the source statement.
- **R15** [claim](#stmt-lem-integer-rows) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-integer-rows): The scaling condition absorbs the integer endpoint contribution and supplies the row bounds required for interpolation. -> The conclusion of the source statement.
- **R16** [claim](#stmt-prop-full-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-full-rank): Row interpolation gives full column rank, hence injectivity contradicts the collision kernel after a common power supplies the scaling condition. -> The conclusion of the source statement.
- **R17** [claim](#stmt-cor-fields) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-fields): Rational incidence minors transfer the simultaneous inequality to uncountable algebraically closed characteristic-zero fields. -> The conclusion of the source statement.
- **R18** [claim](#stmt-cor-sesha) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-sesha): The theorem supplies a strictly nef square-zero divisor class and determines the multipoint Seshadri constant. -> The conclusion of the source statement.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **geometric-scope**: The strict degree inequality holds outside the exceptional union, simultaneously for effective curves and prescribed multiplicities.
- **finite-interpolation**: The row-size distribution conditions suffice to interpolate every function by the prescribed monomial span.
- **rank-persistence**: Full column rank of the limiting matrix implies injectivity of the jet map for sufficiently small positive degeneration parameter.
- **scaling-rank**: The scaling condition absorbs the integer endpoint contribution and yields full column rank of the limiting matrix.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **arbitrary-points**: Apply the strict degree inequality to arbitrary marked points. -> Special configurations need not lie outside the exceptional union.
- **total-count**: Use only the total number of exponent pairs to conclude interpolation. -> A concentrated row or too many large rows can prevent the stated construction.
- **finite-global**: Treat finite interpolation checks as a certificate of the geometric theorem. -> The analytic collision and degeneration arguments are not established by finite evaluations.
- **unscaled-endpoint**: Use the polygon slice estimate without absorbing the integer endpoint. -> The integer row count includes an endpoint contribution.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The horizontal-row lemma gives a finite constructive interpolation procedure; computation is restricted to its finite instances and does not certify the plane-curve theorem.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON cases with positive integers q,m, distinct integer pairs and rational values.
# PRECONDITIONS: q,m positive; pairs distinct; finite rational data; row inequalities checked.
# OUTPUTS: Accepted flag and exact rational coefficients in the allowed monomial span.
# TIME: Polynomially many rational operations in the point count and q*m*m.
# SPACE: Polynomially many rational coefficients in the point count and q*m*m.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact fmpq rational arithmetic; integer coordinates and unbounded integers.
# FAILURE: Malformed input raises an error; violated row inequalities return accepted=false.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpq

def mul(a,b):
    c={}
    for (i,j),v in a.items():
        for (k,l),w in b.items():
            c[i+k,j+l]=c.get((i+k,j+l),fmpq(0))+v*w
    return {key:value for key,value in c.items() if value}

def evaluate(p,j,k):
    return sum((v*fmpq(j)**b*fmpq(k)**l for (b,l),v in p.items()),fmpq(0))

def solve(case):
    q,m=case['q'],case['m']
    assert type(q) is int and type(m) is int and q>0 and m>0
    data=case['points']
    assert all(type(p[0]) is int and type(p[1]) is int for p in data)
    assert len({(p[0],p[1]) for p in data})==len(data)
    rows={}
    for j,k,v in data:
        rows.setdefault(k,[]).append((j,fmpq(str(v))))
    sizes=[len(row) for row in rows.values()]
    allowed=all(n<=m for n in sizes) and all(sum(n>=t for n in sizes)<=q*(m-t+1) for t in range(1,m+1))
    if not allowed:
        return {'accepted':False,'coeffs':[]}
    heights=sorted(rows,key=lambda k:(-len(rows[k]),k))
    polynomial={}
    previous=[]
    for height in heights:
        vanish={(0,0):fmpq(1)}
        for old in previous:
            vanish=mul(vanish,{(0,1):fmpq(1),(0,0):fmpq(-old)})
        scale=evaluate(vanish,0,height)
        correction={}
        row=rows[height]
        for j,target in row:
            basis={(0,0):fmpq(1)}
            denominator=fmpq(1)
            for other,_ in row:
                if other!=j:
                    basis=mul(basis,{(1,0):fmpq(1),(0,0):fmpq(-other)})
                    denominator*=j-other
            residual=target-evaluate(polynomial,j,height)
            factor=residual/(scale*denominator)
            for key,value in basis.items():
                correction[key]=correction.get(key,fmpq(0))+factor*value
        for key,value in mul(vanish,correction).items():
            polynomial[key]=polynomial.get(key,fmpq(0))+value
        previous.append(height)
    coeffs=[[b,l,str(v)] for (b,l),v in sorted(polynomial.items()) if v]
    return {'accepted':True,'coeffs':coeffs}

print(json.dumps([solve(c) for c in json.load(sys.stdin)['cases']],sort_keys=True))
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The constructive row-interpolation proof can be adapted into a symbolic interpolation component whose contract is finite input satisfying both stated row bounds and exact recovery of its target values in the prescribed polynomial span. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source proves geometric multiplicity bounds and finite interpolation; a contract for game state evolution, simulation dynamics, or physical modelling is ABSENT. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The row-interpolation lemma can support a mathematical interpolation checker: test its hypotheses, construct the correction polynomial, and verify its values and allowed monomials. |
| algorithms | True | strong | implementable | inferred | The proof explicitly constructs an interpolant by processing rows in nonincreasing size and adding corrections that vanish on earlier heights. It is executable for exactly represented coordinates satisfying the stated bounds. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Grouping exponent pairs by height and ordering their row sizes supplies a finite organisation for the constructive interpolation procedure. This is an inferred representation contract; the source gives no data-structure efficiency guarantee. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source constructs mathematical evaluation matrices but gives no accelerator architecture, memory-transfer contract, or hardware performance result. An enabling hardware hypothesis is ABSENT. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source concerns effective curves at very general points and interpolation, without a security assumption or adversarial model. A cryptographic reduction is ABSENT. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source interpolates prescribed values on a finite exponent set; a learning objective, sampling model, or generalisation guarantee is ABSENT. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source derives degree exclusions and divisor positivity; an operations-research objective, feasible decision model, or optimisation algorithm is ABSENT. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source uses pointwise existence and numerical matrix limits; a distributed execution model, communication contract, or failure model is ABSENT. |
| other (Algebraic geometry and complex-analytic interpolation) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | The stated application is the strict square-root degree bound for effective plane curves at very general points, together with multipoint positivity on their blowups. The source proof combines normal polynomials, theta sections, collision jets, and finite exponent interpolation. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L-very-general** (hypothesis): The strict inequality concerns tuples outside the stated countable exceptional union; distinctness alone does not establish the hypothesis. Blocked use: ['Applying the inequality to an arbitrary specified tuple of distinct points.']
- **L-endpoint** (edge_case): The strict inequality does not extend to nine points: the source explicitly gives a cubic obstruction. Blocked use: ['Extending the strict inequality to $r=9$.']
- **L-effective** (hypothesis): Degree and multiplicity refer to nonzero homogeneous forms and their effective zero divisors, with repeated components permitted. Blocked use: ['Applying the bound to a zero form or to arbitrary signed divisor classes.']
- **L-field** (hypothesis): The field extension requires an uncountable algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. Blocked use: ['Transferring the field conclusion to positive characteristic, countable fields, or fields that are not algebraically closed.']
- **L-recognition** (nonconstructive): A finite decision procedure recognizing membership outside the countable exceptional union is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Treating very-general existence as an algorithm certifying a supplied point configuration.']
- **L-interpolation** (hypothesis): Finite horizontal-row interpolation requires both the maximum row-size bound and the distribution bound on rows of each minimum size. Blocked use: ['Replacing the row-size hypotheses with only a total cardinality bound.', 'Assuming arbitrary complex input coordinates have an executable exact representation.']
- **L-small-tau** (approximation): The injectivity conclusion is qualitative for sufficiently small positive degeneration parameter; a numerical threshold or stability estimate is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Using an arbitrary positive parameter or an approximate rank computation as the stated injectivity certificate.']
- **L-scaling** (hypothesis): The integer row counts and full-rank conclusion require the scaling condition; the source obtains it by a common integral multiple. Blocked use: ['Omitting the scaling condition for an unscaled degree and multiplicity pair.']
- **L-order** (formal_scope): Collision is taken with the curve fixed before the degeneration limit, and no simultaneous family of kernel vectors is selected. Blocked use: ['Interchanging the collision and degeneration limits without proof.', 'Assuming the proof constructs a continuous family of kernel vectors.']
- **L-engineering** (unknown): A source-supported bridge from geometric degree bounds to correctness of unrelated software operations or physical models is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Certifying unrelated software updates, distributed execution, or physical models from the plane-curve inequality.']
- **L-finite-certificate** (formal_scope): Finite interpolation computations address supplied exponent sets and target values; a finite test is not a certificate of the simultaneous geometric theorem. Blocked use: ['Treating successful interpolation tests as certification of the theorem for every degree and multiplicity vector.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 039 (`lean/docs/039.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Nagata's conjecture for plane curves](../../preprints/Nagatas-Conjecture-for-Plane-Curves-September-23-2026/main.pdf)
> - [Maximal Seshadri constants on arbitrary polarized surfaces](../../preprints/Maximal-Seshadri-Constants-on-Arbitrary-Polarized-Surfaces-September-23-2026/main.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> Nagata's conjecture asserts that a nonzero effective plane curve of degree $d$, with multiplicities at least $m_i$ at $r\ge10$ very general points, satisfies $\sum_i m_i<d\sqrt r$.
>
> The formalization establishes this inequality simultaneously for all curves and multiplicity vectors outside one countable union of proper Zariski-closed exceptional sets with nonempty complement. Reducible curves, repeated components, and unequal multiplicities are included, in both effective-curve and homogeneous-polynomial formulations. The formalization also includes the passage from homogeneous effective cycles to equations.
>
> The maximality question asks whether the multipoint Seshadri constant reaches the upper bound $\sqrt{L^2/r}$ at very general points once $r$ is sufficiently large. The formalized result establishes this for every smooth integral complex projective surface $S$ and ample line bundle $L$.
>
> For every $r$ above a threshold depending on $(S,L)$, it gives a countable union of proper closed exceptional sets with nonempty complement. Outside it, the constant is $\sqrt{L^2/r}$ and the boundary class defining this Seshadri constant on the point blowup is nef. The threshold is not explicit or uniform over all surfaces.

```lean
import Mathlib.Algebra.CharZero.Infinite
import Mathlib.Algebra.Group.Irreducible.Defs
import Mathlib.Algebra.MvPolynomial.Eval
import Mathlib.Algebra.MvPolynomial.NoZeroDivisors
import Mathlib.Algebra.MvPolynomial.Rename
import Mathlib.Analysis.Real.Sqrt
import Mathlib.Basic.Complex.Basic
import Mathlib.Data.Fin.SuccPred
import Mathlib.Data.Fin.Tuple.Basic
import Mathlib.Data.Fintype.BigOperators
import Mathlib.LinearAlgebra.Projectivization.Basic
import Mathlib.RingTheory.GradedAlgebra.Homogeneous.Ideal
import Mathlib.RingTheory.Ideal.Height
import Mathlib.RingTheory.Ideal.IsPrincipal
import Mathlib.RingTheory.Ideal.Maps
import Mathlib.RingTheory.Ideal.Maximal
import Mathlib.RingTheory.Localization.AtPrime.Basic
import Mathlib.RingTheory.MvPolynomial.Homogeneous
import Mathlib.RingTheory.MvPolynomial.Ideal
import Mathlib.RingTheory.Polynomial.UniqueFactorization
import Mathlib.RingTheory.UniqueFactorizationDomain.Basic
import Mathlib.RingTheory.UniqueFactorizationDomain.Finsupp

namespace OAI

/-!
# Nagata's strict inequality for very general complex plane configurations

The exceptional family is chosen before every curve and multiplicity vector.
Closedness means common multihomogeneous zero loci on the product of projective
planes, restricted to the open set of ordered distinct configurations.
-/

noncomputable section

open scoped BigOperators

namespace Nagata.AffineMultiplicity

variable {σ K : Type*} [CommRing K]

def pointIdeal (point : σ → K) : Ideal (MvPolynomial σ K) :=
  RingHom.ker (MvPolynomial.eval point)

def orderAtLeast (point : σ → K) (multiplicity : ℕ)
    (polynomial : MvPolynomial σ K) : Prop :=
  polynomial ∈ pointIdeal point ^ multiplicity

def translateHom (point : σ → K) : MvPolynomial σ K →+* MvPolynomial σ K :=
  MvPolynomial.eval₂Hom MvPolynomial.C
    (fun index => MvPolynomial.X index + MvPolynomial.C (point index))

@[simp] theorem translateHom_C (point : σ → K) (coefficient : K) :
    translateHom point (MvPolynomial.C coefficient) = MvPolynomial.C coefficient := by
  simp [translateHom]

@[simp] theorem translateHom_X (point : σ → K) (index : σ) :
    translateHom point (MvPolynomial.X index) = MvPolynomial.X index + MvPolynomial.C (point index) := by
  simp [translateHom]

theorem translateHom_neg_comp (point : σ → K) :
    (translateHom (-point)).comp (translateHom point) = RingHom.id _ := by
  apply MvPolynomial.ringHom_ext
  · intro coefficient; simp
  · intro index; simp [add_assoc]

theorem translateHom_comp_neg (point : σ → K) :
    (translateHom point).comp (translateHom (-point)) = RingHom.id _ := by
  simpa only [neg_neg] using translateHom_neg_comp (-point)

def translateEquiv (point : σ → K) : MvPolynomial σ K ≃+* MvPolynomial σ K :=
  { translateHom point with
    invFun := translateHom (-point)
    left_inv := fun polynomial => RingHom.congr_fun (translateHom_neg_comp point) polynomial
    right_inv := fun polynomial => RingHom.congr_fun (translateHom_comp_neg point) polynomial }

@[simp] theorem translateEquiv_apply (point : σ → K) (polynomial : MvPolynomial σ K) :
    translateEquiv point polynomial = translateHom point polynomial := rfl

theorem translate_ne_zero (point : σ → K) (polynomial : MvPolynomial σ K)
    (nonzero : polynomial ≠ 0) : translateHom point polynomial ≠ 0 := by
  intro translated_zero
  apply nonzero
  apply (translateEquiv point).injective
  simpa only [translateEquiv_apply, map_zero] using translated_zero

theorem translated_support_nonempty (point : σ → K) (polynomial : MvPolynomial σ K)
    (nonzero : polynomial ≠ 0) : (translateHom point polynomial).support.Nonempty :=
  MvPolynomial.support_nonempty.mpr (translate_ne_zero point polynomial nonzero)

/-- The least total degree of a nonzero Taylor coefficient. -/
def ordinaryMultiplicity (point : σ → K) (polynomial : MvPolynomial σ K)
    (nonzero : polynomial ≠ 0) : ℕ :=
  (translateHom point polynomial).support.inf'
    (translated_support_nonempty point polynomial nonzero) Finsupp.degree

end Nagata.AffineMultiplicity

namespace Nagata.ProjectiveGeometry

abbrev PlanePoint := Projectivization ℂ (Fin 3 → ℂ)

abbrev OrderedDistinctPoints (count : ℕ) :=
  {points : Fin count → PlanePoint // Function.Injective points}

structure MultihomogeneousEquation (count : ℕ) where
  polynomial : MvPolynomial (Fin count × Fin 3) ℂ
  multidegree : Fin count → ℕ
  homogeneous : ∀ exponent ∈ polynomial.support, ∀ index,
    (∑ coordinate : Fin 3, exponent (index, coordinate)) = multidegree index

def equationVanishes {count : ℕ} (equation : MultihomogeneousEquation count)
    (points : Fin count → PlanePoint) : Prop :=
  ∀ (vectors : Fin count → Fin 3 → ℂ) (nonzero : ∀ index, vectors index ≠ 0),
    (∀ index, Projectivization.mk ℂ (vectors index) (nonzero index) = points index) →
    MvPolynomial.eval (fun index => vectors index.1 index.2) equation.polynomial = 0

def IsProductZariskiClosed {count : ℕ} (locus : Set (Fin count → PlanePoint)) : Prop :=
  ∃ equations : Set (MultihomogeneousEquation count),
    ∀ points, points ∈ locus ↔ ∀ equation ∈ equations, equationVanishes equation points

def IsConfigurationZariskiClosed {count : ℕ}
    (locus : Set (OrderedDistinctPoints count)) : Prop :=
  ∃ ambient : Set (Fin count → PlanePoint), IsProductZariskiClosed ambient ∧
    ∀ points, points ∈ locus ↔ points.val ∈ ambient

abbrev ChartVariables (chart : Fin 3) := {coordinate : Fin 3 // coordinate ≠ chart}

def dehomogenize (chart : Fin 3) (polynomial : MvPolynomial (Fin 3) ℂ) :
    MvPolynomial (ChartVariables chart) ℂ :=
  MvPolynomial.eval₂ MvPolynomial.C
    (fun coordinate => if equal : coordinate = chart then 1
      else MvPolynomial.X ⟨coordinate, equal⟩) polynomial

def chartCoordinates (chart : Fin 3) (point : PlanePoint) : ChartVariables chart → ℂ :=
  fun coordinate => point.rep coordinate.val / point.rep chart

/-- Local multiplicity lower bounds are membership in powers of the point ideal. -/
def multiplicityAtLeast (polynomial : MvPolynomial (Fin 3) ℂ)
    (point : PlanePoint) (multiplicity : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∀ chart : Fin 3, point.rep chart ≠ 0 →
    AffineMultiplicity.orderAtLeast (chartCoordinates chart point) multiplicity
      (dehomogenize chart polynomial)

theorem chart_exists (point : PlanePoint) : ∃ chart : Fin 3, point.rep chart ≠ 0 := by
  by_contra absent
  apply point.rep_nonzero
  funext chart
  exact Classical.not_not.mp (fun nonzero => absent ⟨chart, nonzero⟩)

end Nagata.ProjectiveGeometry

namespace Nagata.Workers.W30

open UniqueFactorizationMonoid

abbrev TernaryPolynomial := MvPolynomial (Fin 3) ℂ

local instance : DecidableEq (Associates TernaryPolynomial) := Classical.decEq _

def equationMultiplicity (polynomial : TernaryPolynomial) :
    Associates TernaryPolynomial →₀ ℕ :=
  factorization (Associates.mk polynomial)

def associatePrincipalIdeal (associate : Associates TernaryPolynomial) :
    Ideal TernaryPolynomial :=
  (Ideal.associatesEquivIsPrincipal TernaryPolynomial associate).val

def equationIdealCycle (polynomial : TernaryPolynomial) : Ideal TernaryPolynomial →₀ ℕ :=
  (equationMultiplicity polynomial).mapDomain associatePrincipalIdeal

attribute [local instance] MvPolynomial.gradedAlgebra

theorem exists_positive_degree_equation_of_homogeneous_cycle
    (cycle : Ideal TernaryPolynomial →₀ ℕ) (nonzero : cycle ≠ 0)
    (homogeneous : ∀ ideal ∈ cycle.support,
      ideal.IsPrime ∧ ideal ≠ ⊥ ∧ ideal.IsPrincipal ∧
        ideal.IsHomogeneous (MvPolynomial.homogeneousSubmodule (Fin 3) ℂ)) :
    ∃ polynomial : TernaryPolynomial,
      polynomial ≠ 0 ∧ polynomial.IsHomogeneous polynomial.totalDegree ∧
        0 < polynomial.totalDegree ∧ equationIdealCycle polynomial = cycle := by
  sorry

end Nagata.Workers.W30

namespace Nagata.W16

abbrev PlanePolynomial := MvPolynomial (Fin 3) ℂ

structure NonzeroHomogeneousForm (degree : ℕ) where
  polynomial : PlanePolynomial
  homogeneous : polynomial.IsHomogeneous degree
  nonzero : polynomial ≠ 0

theorem homogeneous_eval_scale {Variables Coefficient : Type*} [CommSemiring Coefficient]
    {polynomial : MvPolynomial Variables Coefficient} {degree : ℕ}
    (homogeneous : polynomial.IsHomogeneous degree)
    (coordinates : Variables → Coefficient) (scalar : Coefficient) :
    MvPolynomial.eval (fun index => scalar * coordinates index) polynomial =
      scalar ^ degree * MvPolynomial.eval coordinates polynomial := by
  classical
  simp only [MvPolynomial.eval_eq, Finset.mul_sum]
  apply Finset.sum_congr rfl
  intro exponent supported
  simp only [mul_pow, Finset.prod_mul_distrib, Finset.prod_pow_eq_pow_sum]
  rw [← homogeneous.degree_eq_sum_deg_support supported]
  ac_rfl

open Nagata.ProjectiveGeometry

theorem eval_dehomogenize (chart : Fin 3) (polynomial : PlanePolynomial)
    (coordinates : ChartVariables chart → ℂ) :
    MvPolynomial.eval coordinates (dehomogenize chart polynomial) =
      MvPolynomial.eval
        (fun index => if equal : index = chart then 1 else coordinates ⟨index, equal⟩)
        polynomial := by
  classical
  unfold dehomogenize
  rw [← MvPolynomial.eval_assoc]
  apply congrArg (fun values : Fin 3 → ℂ => MvPolynomial.eval values polynomial)
  funext index
  by_cases equal : index = chart <;> simp [equal, Function.comp_apply]

theorem eval_dehomogenize_normalized {degree : ℕ} {polynomial : PlanePolynomial}
    (homogeneous : polynomial.IsHomogeneous degree) (chart : Fin 3)
    (coordinates : Fin 3 → ℂ) (chart_nonzero : coordinates chart ≠ 0) :
    MvPolynomial.eval (fun index : ChartVariables chart => coordinates index.val / coordinates chart)
        (dehomogenize chart polynomial) =
      (coordinates chart)⁻¹ ^ degree * MvPolynomial.eval coordinates polynomial := by
  classical
  rw [eval_dehomogenize]
  have normalized :
      (fun index : Fin 3 => if equal : index = chart then (1 : ℂ)
        else coordinates index / coordinates chart) =
        fun index => (coordinates chart)⁻¹ * coordinates index := by
    funext index
    by_cases equal : index = chart
    · subst index
      simp [chart_nonzero]
    · simp [equal, div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm]
  rw [normalized]
  exact homogeneous_eval_scale homogeneous coordinates (coordinates chart)⁻¹

theorem eval_eq_zero_of_dehomogenize_eq_zero {degree : ℕ} {polynomial : PlanePolynomial}
    (homogeneous : polynomial.IsHomogeneous degree) (chart : Fin 3)
    (chart_zero : dehomogenize chart polynomial = 0)
    (coordinates : Fin 3 → ℂ) (chart_nonzero : coordinates chart ≠ 0) :
    MvPolynomial.eval coordinates polynomial = 0 := by
  have evaluated := congrArg
    (MvPolynomial.eval (fun index : ChartVariables chart => coordinates index.val / coordinates chart))
    chart_zero
  rw [eval_dehomogenize_normalized homogeneous chart coordinates chart_nonzero, map_zero] at evaluated
  exact (mul_eq_zero.mp evaluated).resolve_left (pow_ne_zero degree (inv_ne_zero chart_nonzero))

theorem eq_zero_of_dehomogenize_eq_zero {degree : ℕ} {polynomial : PlanePolynomial}
    (homogeneous : polynomial.IsHomogeneous degree) (chart : Fin 3)
    (chart_zero : dehomogenize chart polynomial = 0) : polynomial = 0 := by
  have multiplied : MvPolynomial.X chart * polynomial = 0 := by
    apply ((MvPolynomial.isHomogeneous_X ℂ chart).mul homogeneous).eq_zero_of_forall_eval_eq_zero
    intro coordinates
    rw [MvPolynomial.eval_mul, MvPolynomial.eval_X]
    by_cases chart_nonzero : coordinates chart = 0
    · simp [chart_nonzero]
    · rw [eval_eq_zero_of_dehomogenize_eq_zero homogeneous chart chart_zero coordinates chart_nonzero,
          mul_zero]
  exact (mul_eq_zero.mp multiplied).resolve_left (MvPolynomial.X_ne_zero chart)

theorem dehomogenize_ne_zero {degree : ℕ} {polynomial : PlanePolynomial}
    (homogeneous : polynomial.IsHomogeneous degree) (nonzero : polynomial ≠ 0)
    (chart : Fin 3) : ProjectiveGeometry.dehomogenize chart polynomial ≠ 0 := by
  intro chart_zero
  exact nonzero (eq_zero_of_dehomogenize_eq_zero homogeneous chart chart_zero)

end Nagata.W16

namespace Nagata.Workers.W10

open Nagata.Workers.W30 Nagata.W16

attribute [local instance] MvPolynomial.gradedAlgebra

/-- Nonzero finite cycles of homogeneous prime plane ideals, with natural weights. -/
def EffectivePlaneCurve := {cycle : Ideal TernaryPolynomial →₀ ℕ //
  cycle ≠ 0 ∧ ∀ ideal ∈ cycle.support,
    ideal.IsPrime ∧ ideal ≠ ⊥ ∧ ideal.IsPrincipal ∧
      ideal.IsHomogeneous (MvPolynomial.homogeneousSubmodule (Fin 3) ℂ)}

/-- An equation realizing the entire cycle, including repeated components. -/
def curveEquation (curve : EffectivePlaneCurve) : TernaryPolynomial :=
  Classical.choose (exists_positive_degree_equation_of_homogeneous_cycle
    curve.val curve.property.1 curve.property.2)

theorem curveEquation_spec (curve : EffectivePlaneCurve) :
    curveEquation curve ≠ 0 ∧
      (curveEquation curve).IsHomogeneous (curveEquation curve).totalDegree ∧
      0 < (curveEquation curve).totalDegree ∧ equationIdealCycle (curveEquation curve) = curve.val :=
  Classical.choose_spec (exists_positive_degree_equation_of_homogeneous_cycle
    curve.val curve.property.1 curve.property.2)

theorem curveEquation_nonzero (curve : EffectivePlaneCurve) : curveEquation curve ≠ 0 :=
  (curveEquation_spec curve).1

def curveDegree (curve : EffectivePlaneCurve) : ℕ := (curveEquation curve).totalDegree

theorem curveEquation_homogeneous (curve : EffectivePlaneCurve) :
    (curveEquation curve).IsHomogeneous (curveDegree curve) :=
  (curveEquation_spec curve).2.1

def curveForm (curve : EffectivePlaneCurve) : NonzeroHomogeneousForm (curveDegree curve) :=
  ⟨curveEquation curve, curveEquation_homogeneous curve, curveEquation_nonzero curve⟩

end Nagata.Workers.W10

namespace Nagata.ProjectiveGeometry

def formOrdinaryMultiplicity {degree : ℕ} (form : W16.NonzeroHomogeneousForm degree)
    (point : PlanePoint) : ℕ :=
  let chart := Classical.choose (chart_exists point)
  AffineMultiplicity.ordinaryMultiplicity (chartCoordinates chart point)
    (dehomogenize chart form.polynomial)
    (W16.dehomogenize_ne_zero form.homogeneous form.nonzero chart)

end Nagata.ProjectiveGeometry

namespace Nagata.W15

open Nagata.Workers.W10 Nagata.ProjectiveGeometry

def curveOrdinaryMultiplicity (curve : EffectivePlaneCurve) (point : PlanePoint) : ℕ :=
  formOrdinaryMultiplicity (curveForm curve) point

end Nagata.W15

namespace Nagata

open ProjectiveGeometry

def FullNagata : Prop :=
  ∀ count : ℕ, 10 ≤ count →
    ∃ exceptional : ℕ → Set (OrderedDistinctPoints count),
      (∀ index, IsConfigurationZariskiClosed (exceptional index) ∧
        exceptional index ≠ Set.univ) ∧
      (∃ points : OrderedDistinctPoints count, ∀ index, points ∉ exceptional index) ∧
      ∀ points : OrderedDistinctPoints count, (∀ index, points ∉ exceptional index) →
        ∀ degree : ℕ, 1 ≤ degree →
        ∀ polynomial : MvPolynomial (Fin 3) ℂ,
          polynomial ≠ 0 → polynomial.IsHomogeneous degree →
        ∀ multiplicities : Fin count → ℕ,
          (∀ index, multiplicityAtLeast polynomial (points.val index) (multiplicities index)) →
          (∑ index, (multiplicities index : ℝ)) < (degree : ℝ) * Real.sqrt (count : ℝ)

theorem nagata_conjecture :
    ∀ count : ℕ, 10 ≤ count →
      ∃ exceptional : ℕ → Set (OrderedDistinctPoints count),
        (∀ index, IsConfigurationZariskiClosed (exceptional index) ∧
          exceptional index ≠ Set.univ) ∧
        (∃ points : OrderedDistinctPoints count, ∀ index, points ∉ exceptional index) ∧
        ∀ points : OrderedDistinctPoints count, (∀ index, points ∉ exceptional index) →
          ∀ curve : Workers.W10.EffectivePlaneCurve, ∀ multiplicities : Fin count → ℕ,
            (∀ index, multiplicities index ≤ W15.curveOrdinaryMultiplicity curve (points.val index)) →
            (∑ index, (multiplicities index : ℝ)) <
              (Workers.W10.curveDegree curve : ℝ) * Real.sqrt (count : ℝ) := by
  sorry

theorem nagata_conjecture_coordinates : FullNagata := by
  sorry

end Nagata

end

end OAI
```

```json
    "text": "The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):\n\n- [Nagata's conjecture for plane curves](../../preprints/Nagatas-Conjecture-for-Plane-Curves-September-23-2026/main.pdf)\n- [Maximal Seshadri constants on arbitrary polarized surfaces](../../preprints/Maximal-Seshadri-Constants-on-Arbitrary-Polarized-Surfaces-September-23-2026/main.pdf)\n\n## Scope\n\nNagata's conjecture asserts that a nonzero effective plane curve of degree $d$, with multiplicities at least $m_i$ at $r\\ge10$ very general points, satisfies $\\sum_i m_i<d\\sqrt r$.\n\nThe formalization establishes this inequality simultaneously for all curves and multiplicity vectors outside one countable union of proper Zariski-closed exceptional sets with nonempty complement. Reducible curves, repeated components, and unequal multiplicities are included, in both effective-curve and homogeneous-polynomial formulations. The formalization also includes the passage from homogeneous effective cycles to equations.\n\nThe maximality question asks whether the multipoint Seshadri constant reaches the upper bound $\\sqrt{L^2/r}$ at very general points once $r$ is sufficiently large. The formalized result establishes this for every smooth integral complex projective surface $S$ and ample line bundle $L$.\n\nFor every $r$ above a threshold depending on $(S,L)$, it gives a countable union of proper closed exceptional sets with nonempty complement. Outside it, the constant is $\\sqrt{L^2/r}$ and the boundary class defining this Seshadri constant on the point blowup is nef. The threshold is not explicit or uniform over all surfaces."
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that a nonzero effective plane curve of degree <i>d</i> at <i>r</i> ≥ 10 very general complex points has total multiplicity strictly less than $`d\sqrt r`$. The inequality holds simultaneously for all curves, including reducible and nonreduced curves, and establishes Nagata's conjecture in its strict, nonhomogeneous form.
